M. CONCLUSION 


26. After examining the submissions made by the interested parties and issues raised therein; and considering the 
facts available on record, the Authority concludes that: 


L 


Vi. 


Vil. 


The product under consideration is Seamless tubes, pipes & hollow profiles of iron, alloy or non-alloy 
steel (other than cast iron and stainless steel), whether hot finished or cold drawn or cold rolled of an 
external diameter not exceeding 355.6 mm or |4” OD. It includes boiler pipes or line pipes used in 
hydrocarbon industry, subject to exclusions noted by the Authority. 


The scope of the product under consideration has been curtailed in the present investigation and whereas 
duties were earlier in force on imports of Oil Country Tubular Goods (OCTG) covered under PCNs A- 
-l to A-l-4, the same has been excluded from the scope of the duty in the present review. 


The domestic industry has produced like article to the imported product under consideration. 


The application for initiation of the sunset review of anti-dumping duty was filed by Jindal Saw 
Limited, Kirloskar Ferrous Industries Limited and Maharashtra Seamless Limited. 


The applicants account for 93% of the total Indian production, and thus, constitute domestic industry for 
the like article. 


Considering the normal value and export price determined, the dumping margin for the subject goods 
from the subject country is above de minimis level and is significant. 


The demand for the product has increased over the injury period. The domestic industry holds sufficient 
capacity to meet the present and potential demand for the product. Despite the same, the volume of 
imports have increased over the injury period. 


Despite anti-dumping duties in force, the volume of subject imports has increased in absolute terms and 
in relation to production and consumption in India. 


The imports are predominantly of those categories which forms majority share of the Indian industry 
production profile. 


The domestic industry has suffered injury during the current period - 


a. The subject imports have depressed the prices of the domestic industry in the market. The domestic 
industry has been forced to reduce its prices at a rate much higher than the decline in its costs. 


b. The domestic industry has lost market share and has operated at less than 50% capacity utilisation 
throughout the injury period. 


c. The profitability of the domestic industry has steeply deteriorated over the period. Its profits, cash 
profits and return on capital employed have declined steeply over the years. The return on capital 
employed earned by the domestic industry is quite low and unsustainable for any business to operate 
in long run. 


[भाग I—are ] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 79 


जा, Expiry of anti-dumping duties is likely to result in intensified dumping and consequent injury to the 
domestic industry which is evident from the following. 


a. The volume of imports has continued to enter in increasing volumes despite duties. 


b. The exporters from China PR have continued to export the subject goods at dumped prices over a 
prolonged period. 


c. The subject imports have increased at a rate higher than increase in demand, despite duties. 
d. The exporters in China PR have large surplus capacities, which is higher than Indian demand. 


e. Despite limited domestic demand, the Chinese exporters have established capacities much beyond 
domestic demand. Resultantly, the exporters have no option but to look for global market. 


f. China PR is also the largest exporter of steel and steel products globally. 


g. The exporters in the subject country are facing significant trade remedial measures and other tariff 
measures in various third countries, such as EU, Canada, Brazil, Mexico, Ukraine and the USA. 


h. Exports are being made at a price that is having significant depressing effect on the prices in the 
market. This is likely to intensify in the event of cessation of ADD. 


i. As a result of depressing effects on prices felt by the domestic industry, performance of the 
domestic industry has already deteriorated in respect of profits, cash profits and return on 
investment. The performance is likely to deteriorate further in the event of cessation of ADD. 


xli. Continuation of anti-dumping duty is in the interest of the public and would have no adverse impact on 
the interests of public at large. 


xiii. The product under consideration is not a raw material used in the manufacturing process of any 
downstream product. 


xiv. Continuation of duties would result in negligible increase in the prices of downstream applications and 
would not be a measurable burden for the end-consumers. 


xv. The Indian industry comprises of multiple producers, who have sufficient capacities to meet the current 
and future demand in the country. Thus, continuation of duties would not result in any demand-supply 


gap. 


xvi. Absence of participation by any of the users in the present investigation demonstrates that the anti- 
dumping duties have not adversely impacted the performance of any downstream user. 


N. RECOMMENDATIONS 


27. The Authority notes that the investigation was initiated and notified to all interested parties and adequate 
opportunity was given to the domestic industry, exporters, importers and other interested parties to provide 
positive information on the aspect of continuation / recurrence of dumping and injury in the event of cessation 
of duties. Having concluded that there is likelihood of continuation of dumping and injury to the domestic 
industry if the anti-dumping duties are allowed to cease, the Authority is of the view that continued imposition 
of measures is necessary on imports of the product under consideration from the subject country. 


28. With regard to duty structure, keeping into account factual matrix of the case and having regard to contentions 
raised, information provided and submissions made by interested parties, it is deemed appropriate to 
recommend a fixed form of anti-dumping duty. Accordingly, anti-dumping duty equal to the amount indicated 
in Column (7) of the duty table given below is recommended to be imposed from the date of notification to be 
issued in this regard by the Central Government, on all imports of the product under consideration, from the 
subject country for a further period of five (5) years.
